synthesis     | active       |                                                                                                 |\n132: \n133: ## Header Contents\n134: \n135: This repository currently contains the following header files.\n136: \n137: ### RTL Register Macros\n138: \n139: The header file [`registers.svh`](include/common_cells/registers.svh) contains macros that expand to descriptions of registers.\n140: To avoid misuse of `always_ff` blocks, only the following macros shall be used to describe sequential behavior.\n141: The use of linter rules that flag explicit uses of `always_ff` in source code is encouraged.\n142: \n143: |    Macro     |                             Arguments                             |                                Description                                |\n144: | ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n145: | <code>`FF</code>     | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `rst_val`, (`clk_sig`, `arstn_sig`)             | Flip-flop with asynchronous active-low reset                              |\n146: | <code>`FFAR</code>   | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `arst_sig`                | Flip-flop with asynchronous active-high reset                             |\n147: | <code>`FFARN</code>  | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `arstn_sig`               | *deprecated* Flip-flop with asynchronous active-low reset                 |\n148: | <code>`FFSR</code>   | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `rst_sig`                 | Flip-flop with synchronous active-high reset                              |\n149: | <code>`FFSRN</code>  | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `rstn_sig`                | Flip-flop with synchronous active-low reset                               |\n150: | <code>`FFNR</code>   | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `clk_sig`                                       | Flip-flop without reset                                                   |\n151: |              |                                                                   |                                                                           |\n152: | <code>`FFL</code>    | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `rst_val`, (`clk_sig`, `arstn_sig`) | Flip-flop with load-enable and asynchronous active-low reset              |\n153: | <code>`FFLAR</code>  | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `arst_sig`    | Flip-flop with load-enable and asynchronous active-high reset             |\n154: | <code>`FFLARN</code> | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `arstn_sig`   | *deprecated* Flip-flop with load-enable and asynchronous active-low reset |\n155: | <code>`FFLSR</code>  | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `rst_sig`     | Flip-flop with load-enable and synchronous active-high reset              |\n156: | <code>`FFLSRN</code> | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `rst_val`, `clk_sig`, `rstn_sig`    | Flip-flop with load-enable and synchronous active-low reset               |\n157: | <code>`FFLNR</code>  | `q_sig`, `d_sig`, `load_ena`, `clk_sig`                           | Flip-flop with load-enable without reset                                  |\n158: - *The name of the clock and reset signals for implicit variants is `clk_i` and `rst_ni`, respectively.*\n159: - *Argument suffix `_sig` indicates signal names for present and next state as well as clocks and resets.*\n160: - *Argument `rst_val` specifies the value literal to be assigned upon reset.*\n161: - *Argument `load_ena` specifies the boolean expression that forms the load enable of the register.*\n162: \n163: ### SystemVerilog Assertion Macros\n164: \n165: The header file [`assertions.svh`](include/common_cells/assertions.svh) contains macros that expand to assertion blocks.\n166: These macros should recduce the effort in writing many assertions and make it\n167: easier to use them. They are similar to but incompatible with the macros used by [lowrisc](https://github.com/lowRISC/opentitan/blob/master/hw/ip/prim/rtl/prim_assert.sv).\n168: \n169: #### Simple Assertion and Cover Macros\n170: | Macro              | Arguments                                        | Description                                                                |\n171: | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n172: | <code>`ASSERT_I</code>     | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__desc`)                   | Immediate assertion                                                        |\n173: | <code>`ASSERT_INIT</code>  | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__desc`)                   | Assertion in initial block. Can be used for things like parameter checking |\n174: | <code>`ASSERT_FINAL</code> | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__desc`)                   | Assertion in final block                                                   |\n175: | <code>`ASSERT</code>       | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`) | Assert a concurrent property directly                                      |\n176: | <code>`ASSERT_NEVER</code> | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`) | Assert a concurrent property NEVER happens                                 |\n177: | <code>`ASSERT_KNOWN</code> | `__name`, `__sig`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`)  | Concurrent clocked assertion with custom error message                     |\n178: | <code>`COVER</code>        | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`)           | Cover a concurrent property                                                |\n179: - *The name of the clock and reset signals for implicit variants is `clk_i` and `rst_ni`, respectively.*\n180: - *`__desc` is an optional string argument describing the failure causing the assertion to be violated that is embedded into the error report and defaults to `\"\"`.*\n181: \n182: #### Complex Assertion Macros\n183: | Macro                 | Arguments                                                                           | Description                                                                                                |\n184: | --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n185: | <code>`ASSERT_PULSE</code>    | `__name`, `__sig`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`)                                     | Assert that signal is an active-high pulse with pulse length of 1 clock cycle                              |\n186: | <code>`ASSERT_IF</code>       | `__name`, `__prop`, `__enable`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`)                        | Assert that a property is true only when an enable signal is set                                           |\n187: | <code>`ASSERT_KNOWN_IF</code> | `__name`, `__sig`, `__enable`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`)                         | Assert that signal has a known value (each bit is either '0' or '1') after reset if enable is set          |\n188: | <code>`ASSERT_STABLE</code>   | `__name`, `__valid`, `__ready`, `__data`, `__enable`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`)  | Assert that the data on a ready-valid interface is kept stable after valid is asserted, until ready is too |\n189: - *The name of the clock and reset signals for implicit variants is `clk_i` and `rst_ni`, respectively.*\n190: - *`__desc` is an optional string argument describing the failure causing the assertion to be violated that is embedded into the error report and defaults to `\"\"`.*\n191: \n192: #### Assumption Macros\n193: \n194: | Macro          | Arguments                                        | Description                  |\n195: | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------- |\n196: | <code>`ASSUME</code>   | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`) | Assume a concurrent property |\n197: | <code>`ASSUME_I</code> | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__desc`)                   | Assume an immediate property |\n198: - *The name of the clock and reset signals for implicit variants is `clk_i` and `rst_ni`, respectively.*\n199: - *`__desc` is an optional string argument describing the failure causing the assertion to be violated that is embedded into the error report and defaults to `\"\"`.*\n200: \n201: #### Formal Verification Macros\n202: \n203: | Macro              | Arguments                                        | Description                                                  |\n204: | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------ |\n205: | <code>`ASSUME_FPV</code>   | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`, `__desc`) | Assume a concurrent property during formal verification only |\n206: | <code>`ASSUME_I_FPV</code> | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__desc`)                   | Assume a concurrent property during formal verification only |\n207: | <code>`COVER_FPV</code>    | `__name`, `__prop`, (`__clk`, `__rst`)           | Cover a concurrent property during formal verification       |\n208: - *The name of the clock and reset signals for implicit variants is `clk_i` and `rst_ni`, respectively.*\n209: - *`__desc` is an optional string argument describing the failure causing the assertion to be violated that is embedded into the error report and defaults to `\"\"`.*\n\n(End of file - The only difference to a regular multiplexer cell is that they\n// feature balanced rise- and fall-times. In other words: SWITCHING FROM ONE\n// CLOCK TO THE OTHER CAN INTRODUCE GLITCHES. ALSO, GLITCHES ON THE SELECT LINE\n// DIRECTLY TRANSLATE TO GLITCHES ON THE OUTPUT CLOCK!! This cell is only\n// intended to be used for quasi-static switching between clocks when one of the\n// clocks is anyway inactive or if the downstream logic remains gated or in\n// reset state during the transition phase. It indicates wether this particular clk gate instance is\n  /// required for functional correctness or just instantiated for power\n  /// savings. If IS_FUNCTIONAL == 0, technology specific mappings might\n  /// replace this cell with a feedthrough connection without any gating.\n  parameter bit IS_FUNCTIONAL = 1'b1\n)(\n   input  logic clk_i,\n   input  logic en_i,\n   input  logic test_en_i,\n   output logic clk_o\n);\n\n  logic clk_en;\n\n  always_latch begin\n    if (clk_i == 1'b0) clk_en <= en_i | test_en_i;\n  end\n\n  assign clk_o = clk_i & clk_en;\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tc_clk_inverter (\n  input  logic clk_i,\n  output logic clk_o\n);\n\n  assign clk_o = ~clk_i;\n\nendmodule\n\n// Warning: Typical clock mux cells of a technologies std cell library ARE NOT\n// GLITCH FREE!! The only difference to a regular multiplexer cell is that they\n// feature balanced rise- and fall-times. In other words: SWITCHING FROM ONE\n// CLOCK TO THE OTHER CAN INTRODUCE GLITCHES. ALSO, GLITCHES ON THE SELECT LINE\n// DIRECTLY TRANSLATE TO GLITCHES ON THE OUTPUT CLOCK!! This cell is only\n// intended to be used for quasi-static switching between clocks when one of the\n// clocks is anyway inactive or if the downstream logic remains gated or in\n// reset state during the transition phase. If you need dynamic switching\n// between arbitrary input clocks without introducing glitches, have a look at\n// the clk_mux_glitch_free cell in the pulp-platform/common_cells repository.\nmodule tc_clk_mux2 (\n  input  logic clk0_i,\n  input  logic clk1_i,\n  input  logic clk_sel_i,\n  output logic clk_o\n);\n\n  assign clk_o = (clk_sel_i) ? clk1_i : clk0_i;\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tc_clk_xor2 (\n  input  logic clk0_i,\n  input  logic clk1_i,\n  output logic clk_o\n);\n\n  assign clk_o = clk0_i ^ clk1_i;\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tc_clk_or2 (\n  input logic clk0_i,\n  input logic clk1_i,\n  output logic clk_o\n);\n\n","exit":0,"description":"Read tech_cells_generic tc_clk.sv","truncated":false},"title":"Read tech_cells_generic tc_clk.sv","time":{"start":1777941302131,"end":1777941302134}},"metadata":{"openrouter":{"reasoning_details":[{"type":"reasoning.text","text":"Let me continue implementing more modules. Calculates overall parity bit at position d + p = 71\n\nMy SystemVerilog implementation does the same thing, but with 0-indexed positions (position 0 = 1-indexed position 1, etc.).\n\nThe mapping is:\n- C codeword[i-1] = SV codeword[i-1] (both use 0-indexed arrays)\n- C's ispowerof2(i) checks if i (1-indexed) is a power of 2\n- My is_power_of_2[i] also checks if i (1-indexed) is a power of 2\n\nWait, looking at the C code:\n```c\nfor (int i = 1, idx = 0; i <= d + p; i++) {\n    codeword[i - 1] = (ispowerof2(i)) ? 0 : info[idx++];\n}\n```\n\nAnd my SV code:\n```systemverilog\nfor (int i = 1; i <= D + P; i++) begin\n    if (!is_power_of_2[i]) begin\n        codeword[i-1] = data_i[idx];\n        idx++;\n    end\nend\n```\n\nThis looks correct - the same logic.\n\nFor parity calculation:\n```c\nfor (int i = 0; i < p; i++) {\n    int parity = 0;\n    for (int k = 1; k <= d + p; k++) {\n        if (k & (1 << i))\n            parity ^= codeword[k - 1];\n    }\n    codeword[(1 << i) - 1] = parity;\n}\n```\n\nAnd my SV code:\n```systemverilog\nfor (int i = 0; i < P; i++) begin\n    automatic logic parity = 0;\n    for (int k = 1; k <= D + P; k++) begin\n        if (k & (1 << i)) begin\n            parity = parity ^ codeword[k-1];\n        end\n    end\n    codeword[(1 << i) - 1] = parity;\nend\n```\n\nThis also looks correct.\n\nFor the overall parity:\n```c\ncodeword[d + p] = 0;\nfor (int i = 0; i < d + p; i++) codeword[d + p] ^= codeword[i];\n```\n\nAnd my SV code:\n```systemverilog\ncodeword[D + P] = '0;\nfor (int i = 0; i < D + P; i++) begin\n    codeword[D + P] = codeword[D + P] ^ codeword[i];\nend\n```\n\nThis also looks correct.\n\nSo the algorithm should be correct.
